The outfit's design is wrapped around the entire suit as one continuous print from the collar to the ankles, wrapping around the sides to the back. The print shows a fitted tunic, tights, with a plain hood printed in the outfit's colours, ballet slippers printed on the feet, and printed gloves on the attached gloves at the sleeve ends. The back of the suit shows the matching back of the outfit, printed edge to edge, from the collar or hood to the ankles or feet. No background colour or plain jersey shows around the print, on the shoulders, sleeves or legs. The outfit's cloth wraps the whole suit like a pattern, with the body, the outside and inside of each sleeve and each leg, and the sides, so there are no plain coloured side panels or background patches anywhere. There are no printed arms, hands, fingers, legs or feet and no printed skin on the suit, except a small printed neckline detail where the outfit is open. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The outfit's collar meets the real collar band, its shoulders sit on the real shoulder seams and its sleeves fill the real sleeves, all in one piece of printed cloth. The silhouette is a plain one-piece suit, with every part of the outfit printed inside its shape and none beyond it. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided suit shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. The hood carries only hood cloth, no face, hair, ears or head is printed on it, and no crowd, audience, stage, lights or scene appears anywhere on the suit, only the outfit's own cloth. No limbs are printed on the suit, bare arms and legs are never drawn as skin on the fabric, and the suit is simply cut short or sleeveless wherever the outfit would show them. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the suit is outfit cloth. The outline is exactly a onesie's, nothing sticks out past the suit's edges, and no coat tails, skirts, capes, extra sleeves or loose layers hang outside it. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided suit shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. The hood carries only hood cloth, no face, hair, ears or head is printed on it, and no crowd, audience, stage, lights or scene appears anywhere on the suit, only the outfit's own cloth. The print shows a bare torso, a mawashi belt, with a topknot printed on the hood, with no footwear shown because the suit is footless, and the sleeves end in plain cuffs with no hands printed. The back of the suit shows the matching back of the outfit, printed edge to edge, from the collar or hood to the ankles or feet. No background colour or plain jersey shows around the print, on the shoulders, sleeves or legs. The outfit's cloth wraps the whole suit like a pattern, with the body, the outside and inside of each sleeve and each leg, and the sides, so there are no plain coloured side panels or background patches anywhere. No limbs are printed on the suit, bare arms and legs are never drawn as skin on the fabric, and the suit is simply cut short or sleeveless wherever the outfit would show them. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The print is cut exactly to the suit, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The silhouette is a plain one-piece suit, with every part of the outfit printed inside its shape and none beyond it. The outfit is stretched to the suit's own straight-sided shape and fills it completely, so it suits any body size. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. It is not a drawing of a person standing in the middle of the suit, no body, no head, no limbs and no figure are pictured, only the outfit's cloth, trims and fastenings spread over the whole suit. A printed waistband showing only the top of a mawashi belt. Raised plush layers are sewn on over the print, the epaulettes in faux leather, the pockets in fleece and the straps and holsters in chenille, standing out from the surface. A printed base shows the full outfit, while felt, fleece and faux-leather pieces are stitched over the key details to give them real depth. A full sumo wrestler costume, head to toe in a single suit, with a bare torso, a mawashi belt on the legs, a topknot (as the hood), sandals (as the feet). Nothing of the outfit is missing and the legs of the suit show the lower garment. Novelty embellishments, mirrored sequin stars and shapes, pocket flaps and straps added just for fun, quilted puffy panels on the chest and dangling fabric tassels on the zip pull and hood. The embellishments are sewn on as real raised fabric, so the suit has depth, texture and sparkle from every angle.
